# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.
### 13.7 Wave-19 correction: admissible starts and persistent refutations

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-19. This section supersedes conflicting seed-only,
fixture-selection and bounded-check wording above. The coordinator explicitly
commissions this semantic correction after wave-19 day-two evidence.

An absent or row-empty fixture selection uses generated security populations;
an empty directory is not an authored population. Invalid/nonempty explicit
fixtures still refuse. Business-case and saved-source selection retain their
separate meanings. Authored fixture assertions and admitted row-read witnesses
fail even without an authored initialization trace. They are trusted starting
assumptions, not synthetic writes. Arbitrarily invented schema snapshots and
unreachable synthetic writes remain supplemental. Column disclosure still needs
an actual authored observer; raw whole-row admission cannot refute a projected
private field.

Generated candidates are proposals, not trusted fixtures. Bootstrap roster rows
and tables without an authored creator must satisfy an ordinary insert gate for
an identified actor, defaults and all constraints. Where authored creators
exist, generate initial payloads by executing those creators with real typed
arguments and uploaded media, preserving their requires, copied relationships,
constant/default fields and role restrictions. Never independently randomize
values a creator couples. Unsupported creators/populations name their gap;
missing coverage is not proof. Actual later role changes and relationship
transfers retain their committed steps and generated or authored fixture origin.
Historical grants are not silently repaired to the current role/relationship.

The bounded check cannot promise to rediscover every full campaign witness.
`test` records only its concrete refutations and completion metadata, keyed to
all selected source bytes, fixture input bytes and the exact CLI executable.
`check` incorporates matching refutations into its ordinary failing summary;
changed source, fixtures or binary invalidate the record. No cached passing
verdict establishes proof. Persistence failure is explicit. The record lives
with generated project artifacts, not a deployed store or authentication state.

Every escalation note remains visible in short output even beyond the ordinary
five-note cap. Risk names prefer the role conferring the largest established
surface, without relying on names such as admin. Check prints a short exposure
line independent of N_ note rendering. A rule-changing deploy --check explicitly
demands `snapback4 test` when no matching completed campaign exists, including
when no independent invariants are declared. It prints the short exposure
summary; it must not silently imply that changed rules were security-tested.
The demand is actionable preflight output, not a new prohibition on activation.

Negative-existence column policies use actual current snapshot truth, never
negate an unavailable/unknown probe into a refutation. Column update intent
observes only actual changes to that column; changing a sibling is not evidence.
Finite campaign caps and unknown stability boundaries remain explicit. Tests
must cover the wave-19 examples (041 mismatched grant role, 044 assignee,
034 judge/coach overlap, 047 cold-approval state, 019 copied immutable owner),
not suppress findings by weakening independent intent.


Wave-19 review clarifications (controlling): the coordinator explicitly requires
catches from the app's own seed/fixtures to fail again. An authored stale seed
therefore fails under its trusted starting-state assumption; it is never
credited as an observed role-change or transfer. Credit for 019's transition
coverage requires the committed role/relationship mutation in the same trace,
followed by the actual forbidden observer. Generated initial states never gain
that credit merely by arranging stale fields. Partial nonempty authored fixtures
are retained as authored input without silently supplementing invented rows;
row-empty inputs use generation, and missing populations remain named coverage.

Creator discovery uses the bound mutation IR's insert/upsert write census. Its
actual interpreter executes require predicates and copied fields; no symbolic
coupling guess admits a generated payload. Global or explicitly role-bearing
roster proposals and tables without an authored creator require a specific
insert-admitted actor in the pre-insert world; a candidate cannot authorize its
own bootstrap. Text arguments include literals compared in bound binary
expressions (including require role='operator'). Generated starting states are
still explicitly labeled; initialization is never silently called empty-start.

The record covers the fixed full-default security campaign (512 single/pair
history envelope, existing full observer/matrix caps and clock NOW), not a
caller-selected bounded campaign. Exact executable identity binds these
constants and their algorithms; source and sorted fixture names/contents bind
inputs. JSON, verbosity, business-case flags and sweep --as do not change this
security campaign; residency selections do not write records; saved-source tests bind selected
source and contemporary fixture bytes separately from the working source.
Records include attempted/sequence counts and coverage-gap counts. Completion
means all finite security phases returned, not exhaustive coverage or proof;
authentication-boundary failure cannot produce completed evidence. No passing
status is loaded into static proof. Write failure is explicit and leaves the
preview demanding a campaign. Records are generated/security-test.json under
the app's snapback directory; temp-file rename avoids partial reads.

Changed/new/deleted table rules count as rule changes, comparing all four bound
gates against the currently selected release. Preview prints the demand even
with zero invariants; it does not veto actual activation. SEC4 also surfaces
this reminder on ordinary deploy. The short exposure line states table,
conditional-audience and broad-read counts independently of N_ note rendering;
audit retains exact per-role and per-column predicates. Most-privileged naming
sorts candidate roles by the number of table/effect gates proved universally
admitted after substituting that role, descending, then deterministic role
order; the source location, effective gate and recipe remain in the note.
The existing 200-schema false-refusal bar and explicit origin labels remain.

Only failure-eligible origins are persisted or reloaded, with their exact origin
and witness. Supplemental synthetic observations are never promoted by caching.
A later witness-free or interrupted run on identical inputs does not erase
established failures: records merge prior eligible witnesses, deduplicating by
site/observer/viewer/effect/origin; a source, fixture or executable change is
the invalidation boundary. Full phase completion and known violations are
separate metadata. Saved-source deploys cannot borrow the working-source record;
the demand must name the corresponding `test --source <candidate> --fixture ...`
selection, and use matching selected-source evidence, never the working-source record.

Distinct committed operation/viewer paths are not collapsed merely because
their lengths match. A one-step foundUnit witness cannot hide registerStaff,
and a one-step provision cannot hide linkUnit. Deduplication preserves each
operation/viewer path; repeated values on that path remain finite sampling.
A declined duplicate never mutates a previous witness's operation or arguments.
Relationship candidates prioritize actual admitted principal transfers from the
initial world, with explicit bounded probing, followed by ordinary replay and
observation. No preflight-only candidate is itself a refutation.

Retained-grant lifetime advice no longer requires declared role intent when a
grant writer depends on a mutable role roster but the read branch does not
consult that roster. Its wording is conditional: grants can survive role
changes; confirm whether current-role intent is required. This does not infer
a privacy specification or refute an invariant. It restores 019's pointer while
keeping lifetime advice out of the escalation/failing-witness tier.

A bounded check with matching known failures may skip additional concrete
resampling. It still runs static analysis and advice, incorporates the eligible
witnesses, names the skipped coverage and fails. Other obligations stay unproved;
full audit/test retain their campaigns. This avoids paying both executable
fingerprinting and exploratory work merely to rediscover a known failure.

Counterfactual column worlds must also respect authored coupling. A field
produced only by copied/computed/constant writer expressions, or by an argument
that is additionally used in a guard/another expression, is not independently
perturbed. The paired reduction stays uncovered unless a bound writer supplies
it from an independently used typed argument. Actual compiler-typed payloads
and committed transition observations still run and can fail. In particular,
changing immutable reports.homeowner while leaving its source chimney.owner
fixed cannot make a safe self-filtered query appear to leak a private field.

Records bind the bytes actually consumed, not a fresh read after testing. Source
strings are captured before compilation. Selected fixture inputs, including
symlink targets followed by the loader, are copied once into private disposable
scratch and both business/security phases consume that copy. The key is derived
from those captured source/fixture bytes. Before publishing the record, compare
the current original selection to that key; a change is explicit stale-input
evidence and does not persist. Later fixture symlink-target changes invalidate
records exactly like ordinary file edits. Scratch is removed on every return.

Writer-object coupling follows compiler Const/Let aliases (ordinary inserts
write a temporary, not an inline object). Unknown/reassigned object aliases are
not independent column worlds. Raw trusted row admission needs one witness per
declaration, not a full cloned initial snapshot for every row. Persisted evidence
deduplicates on the first write as well as later merges, keeping the complete
chosen initial world. A successfully stored matching refutation is never silently
ignored because its JSON exceeds a size threshold. The 600-row regression retains
all initial rows in a sub-megabyte record and check still fails on that evidence.

Guard dependence includes aliases of inserted rows and subsequent reads of the
written table, including post-insert require/check rollback. An argument's
single textual occurrence does not establish independence when a guard observes
its stored value through such an alias; unresolved guard aliases stay uncovered.
Packed qualification requires an actual policy or isolated HTTP challenge,
records the two counts separately, and retains explicit native campaign gaps
(e.g. the existing unsupported ephemeral-hub fork) rather than claiming native
mutation coverage from HTTP probes.

Packed social qualification exposed a complete native campaign skip when an
otherwise durable app merely declared an ephemeral table. Fixture-only forks
now copy the hub under its lock into independent memory, preserving row/index
state, deadlines, per-principal/global quota accounting and limits, epoch, ring
and generation binding. The copy never retains the live publisher or external
presence. A mismatched hub/store generation refuses. These are quiescent test
fixture copies, not live adoption or wire changes (LLP3000.017 §§3–4).
Durable generated initialization and histories then run normally in mixed apps.
Generated ephemeral populations and ephemeral mutation histories remain explicit
coverage gaps; their mutation programs are skipped rather than dispatched
through the durable mutation door. External presence still refuses a fixture
fork. Packed social must exercise actual native challenges, not a zero-work pass.

The unsupported ephemeral-program boundary applies in every dispatch phase,
including column/row paired observers and foreign-asset challenges, with a
central observer refusal as a backstop. No phase credits a skipped ephemeral
mutation as a reachable witness. Durable query controls still execute.
Column independence follows aliases into other written fields/tables as well
as guards. A creator copying an inserted private value into a related marker
constrains the alternate world; changing only the source value cannot turn
an always-public id list into a column disclosure. Alias-dependent operators
and opaque effects likewise stay conservatively uncovered.

Alias dependence is field-sensitive across compiler-owned object projections:
a copied row id does not couple an independently supplied private payload.
Unknown, defaulted or implicit upsert field sources remain uncovered. An
immutable-column alternate that must rebuild the SQL fixture declines when
the original hub has committed ephemeral state; it must not reset that state
and attribute the resulting public-presence difference to a protected column.
Actual typed disclosures and state-preserving forks remain available.

Roster discovery reuses the authority classifier's global principal/role index
relationship. A payload column merely named `role` does not bypass its authored
creator; drafts/final reports preserve creator constants and integrity checks.
The ephemeral exclusion also applies to operation-specific synthetic probes.
HTTP checks recognize existing route-specific authentication refusals: queries
and durable mutations use401 denied.E_AUTH; declared ephemeral mutations may
use200 failed/why.E_AUTH. Both require the auth family, no application-rule
marker and no successful data/result. Apply this equally to unsigned/forged
requests and actually retired credentials. Live wire semantics do not change.

The generated roster bootstrap exemption additionally requires an actual
viewer/role authority probe in a schema gate. A principal key alone does not
make a `role` or `kind` payload field an authority seat; such created tables
execute their authored creator. Program-only authority whose bootstrap cannot
be established within those creators is left explicitly unpopulated rather
than inventing payload states. Role actor proposals remain available.
## 2. The authoring surface

### 2.1 Declaration

`ephemeral <life>` is a line in a table body, beside `expire at`, `sync` and
`online only`. `<life>` uses the duration units that schedules already
accept (`500ms`, `4s`, `1m`). It is required and must be between 1 second
and 60 seconds inclusive. Outside that range the compiler refuses with
`E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`, and the message states the range.

The table keeps the ordinary body grammar: columns, `unique`, `by`, `cap`,
`immutable`, `read`/`insert`/`update`/`delete` rules and `after insert`. The
implicit `id` column and `byId` stay. `default now` columns are allowed.
Some clauses cannot appear in an ephemeral table, because each would make
durable state depend on memory. The compiler refuses them with
`E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`, and the message names the clause and the fix:

| Refused on an ephemeral table | Why | Fix the message gives |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sync …`, `online only`, `sync public`, a horizon | The rows never reach a device | Delete the line; devices read ephemeral rows from the server |
| `expire at .col` | Its life is the declaration | Delete it; `ephemeral <life>` sets every row's deadline |
| `sequence by`, `through`, `words(..)` indexes, asset columns (`image`, `video`), `once` | Durable custody | Keep that data in a durable table |
| Being the source of a `maintain`, a Following view or a feed | Durable derived data | Count or rank durable rows |

Nothing may depend on ephemeral rows except a query's own reads. The
compiler refuses each of these with `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`:

- **any** table's rule or `after insert` that probes an ephemeral table
  (`exists typing[..]`), durable or ephemeral. An ephemeral row's
  visibility then depends only on its own deadline and on durable rows,
  which is what lets deadline expiry stay silent (§3.3);
- a column of any table whose type is an ephemeral table (a reference);
- `sync to typing[..]`;
- a durable mutation, job, schedule or action that reads or writes an
  ephemeral table;
- a `held` query that reads an ephemeral table (held means answered by the
  device).

This follows LLP 3000.009 §7: "Ephemeral state never decides a durable
invariant."

### 2.2 Ephemeral mutations

The binder classifies each mutation by the tables it writes and reads,
using an **exhaustive effect visitor**. `ir::write_tables` is not one: it
misses writes inside expressions such as `Expr::Block` (reviewed probe). The
visitor walks statements, nested expressions, loop domains and inlined
helpers.

- **Durable:** writes no ephemeral table and reads none. Unchanged.
- **Ephemeral:** writes only ephemeral tables. It may read durable tables,
  for example with `require exists members[roomId, viewer] else NOT_MEMBER`,
  and it may use `insert`, `upsert`, `update`, `delete`, `if absent`,
  `require`, `let` and `return`. It may not `emit`, call a native job, use a
  sequence, or write a durable table.
- **Mixed:** writes both kinds. Refused with `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`: "split
  this into two mutations; call the ephemeral one from the client after the
  durable one".

A runtime boundary backs the classification. While an ephemeral mutation
runs, the layered row store (§4.3) refuses any durable write with an
internal refusal and rolls back. A durable mutation's store has no catalog
entry for an ephemeral table, so it cannot write one either.

The binder also marks an ephemeral mutation **`renews`** only when it can
prove that running it twice in a row has the same effect and result as
running it once. The proof is conservative:

- the program makes exactly one write, an `upsert` or `update` of an
  ephemeral table;
- that write's key and every patch value are built only from arguments,
  `viewer`, literals and `now`, never from a read (for example, no
  `n: old.n + 1`);
- anything before the write is a `require` guard, or a `let` used only by
  guards; those guards may read durable tables only, since an ephemeral
  write may invalidate an ephemeral guard;
- the program returns nothing.

`setTyping` renews. None of these does: `stopTyping`, an insert of a
reaction burst, a counter or toggle, or any operation that returns a value.
(Revision 3: Astra's delta probe compiled an upsert keyed by `viewer` that
patched `n: old.n + 1`. It met revision 2's key-only criterion but was not a
renewal.) Only `renews` operations are coalesced or suppressed by the
client (§2.4). All others are sent once per call, in order.

The classification appears in three places:

- `check`'s program listing, as `ephemeral` or `ephemeral, renews`;
- `check --residency`, where each ephemeral table is listed as
  `ephemeral <life>: server memory, never on a device`;
- the generated operation (§2.4).

An ephemeral mutation's semantics:

- **Life.** Each insert or upsert-insert of an ephemeral row stamps the
  deadline `now + life`. Each update, and each upsert that patches an
  existing row, stamps it again, even with an empty patch. That renewal is
  what a heartbeat means. `$deadline` is stamped as its own assignment:
  `recompute_deadline` returns early without `through`, and `merge_patch`
  copies the old deadline. A delete removes the row.
- **The renewal law.** Any write that stamps `$deadline` must pass the
  table's **insert** rule on the new image, in addition to whatever rule
  the write already needs (update, or insert on the insert arm). Extending a
  row's life re-admits it. So a principal who loses the right to insert,
  for example a removed member, cannot keep a row alive past one life,
  whatever the update rule says. Refusal is the update's ordinary refusal
  (`E_RULE` / `NOT_FOUND` per the writes card's table).
- **Rules.** Read, insert, update and delete rules are checked exactly as
  for durable tables. Durable tables are read at the reader's snapshot.
  Ephemeral tables are read at the memory store's state under its lock
  (§3.4 fixes the order).
- **Failure.** The mutation applies completely or not at all. A drop guard
  undoes the journal on every exit that is not a completed commit, including
  refusals, store errors and panics.
- **Result.** The result is the program's return value. There is no `seq`,
  no replay and no id-based idempotence. The client-minted `id` is only a
  correlation token. The server mints any new row ids itself and ignores
  `newIds`.
- **Unique keys, caps and constraints** apply within the memory store. An
  expired row does not collide, as with `expire at`.

### 2.3 Queries over ephemeral tables

A query reads an ephemeral table like any other:

```quarry
query typingIn(roomId: rooms):
  return typing where .roomId = roomId and .principal != viewer by .principal { principal }
```

The query runs on the server. On a device, a read that touches an ephemeral
table is `unknown`, and the client's existing server fallback answers it.
Answers carry `nextExpiry`, the earliest deadline among the ephemeral rows
the answer read.

**An ephemeral read is always its own query.** Reading `typing` inside a
query makes the whole query server-answered: a table without `sync` gives a
server site (`bind/residency.rs:209`), and any server site makes the query
`runs: "server"` (`residency.rs:122`). Joining typing into the chats list
would take the list off the device, so M6's cold-offline chats list would
break, and every typing write would re-run every user's chats list on the
server. `check` therefore prints a note, `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER`, when a query
that would otherwise run on the device runs on the server only because it
reads an ephemeral table. The note's rewrite is: split the ephemeral read
into its own query and render it next to the device-held one.

The `ephemeral` card teaches the chats-list hint as a separate query,
rendered beside the device-held list:

```quarry
query typingInMyRooms():
  return typing where exists members[.roomId, viewer] and .principal != viewer first 100 by .roomId { roomId, principal }
```

The lane chooses the exact spelling the language accepts (an indexed scan
admitted by the read rule, or a nested read per membership). The suite
compiles the card and runs it.

### 2.4 The client

**Generated facts.** For an ephemeral mutation, the generated operation
carries:
- `ephemeral: true`;
- `renews: true` when the binder marked it;
- `renewMs`: a quarter of the shortest life among the tables it writes, in
  milliseconds.

For every query, the generated operation carries `ephemeralReads`: the
static list of ephemeral tables its read closure can touch (read sites,
helpers, and native reads treated conservatively), omitted when empty.

The same facts appear on the operation entries of `/schema`.

```ts
export const setTyping: Ephemeral<{ roomId: Id<"rooms"> }, null> =
  { kind: "mutation", name: "setTyping", runs: "server", predicts: "never", ephemeral: true, renews: true, renewMs: 1000 };
export const typingIn: Query<{ roomId: Id<"rooms"> }, { principal: Principal }[]> =
  { kind: "query", name: "typingIn", runs: "server", ephemeralReads: ["typing"] };
```

**Types.** Durable and ephemeral mutations are disjoint types over a common
base, so widening cannot pick the wrong overload:

```ts
export type Mutation<A, R> = Op<A, R> & { readonly kind: "mutation"; readonly ephemeral?: false };
export type Ephemeral<A, R> = Op<A, R> & { readonly kind: "mutation"; readonly ephemeral: true;
  readonly renews?: true; readonly renewMs: number };
export type Signal<R = unknown> =
  | { readonly state: "sent"; readonly id: string; readonly result: R }
  | { readonly state: "failed"; readonly id: string; readonly why: Refusal };
```

`client.mutate(op, args)` resolves to `Signal<R>` for an `Ephemeral` and to
`Write<R>` for a `Mutation`. It takes no `MutateOptions` for an ephemeral
operation. `useMutation(op)` keeps its `{ run, last, inFlight }` shape and
types `run`'s result the same way. `usePendingWrite` accepts only
`Mutation`. Negative type tests pin all of this.

**Transport.** Calls travel the ordinary `POST /m/<name>` route, but they
bypass everything durable:

- the device outbox and its admission journal;
- the online tier's pending queue;
- `app-online.ts`'s durable journal;
- the browser admission path in `app-client.ts`;
- prediction.

**The ephemeral lane.** Each client has one ephemeral lane. It keeps at most
one request in flight and sends calls in call order. Keeping calls in order
means a `stopTyping` can never be overtaken by an earlier `setTyping`.

1. **Coalescing, `renews` operations only.**
   - The key is the operation name plus the canonical JSON of its arguments.
   - A new `renews` call whose key matches a call still waiting in the lane
     removes that waiting call and joins the end of the lane. The removed
     call's promise resolves with the new call's outcome.
   - A call that is already in flight is never replaced.
   - Non-`renews` operations are never coalesced.
2. **Renewal window, `renews` operations only.**
   - A call is not sent, and resolves at once with the previous result, if
     all of these hold:
     - its key equals that of the lane's last successfully sent call;
     - that call was sent less than `renewMs` ago;
     - the lane has sent no other call since, and none is waiting.
   - The rule is applied both when the call is made and when it reaches the
     head of the lane.
   - The skipped call's row is still alive for at least three quarters of
     its life.
   - A keystroke-per-call typing indicator therefore sends about once per
     `renewMs`, and its row expires between `0.75 × life` and `life` after
     the last keystroke.
3. **Offline.**
   - While the client's link is not `online`, a call resolves at once with
     `{ state: "failed", why: E_OFFLINE }`. It is never queued or retried.
   - When the link goes offline, every waiting call fails the same way.
   - `E_OFFLINE`'s card text gains that sentence: an ephemeral write is
     dropped, not held.
4. **Bounded.**
   - The lane holds at most 256 waiting calls.
   - Beyond that, a new call fails locally with `E_RATE_LIMIT` and is not
     sent.
5. **Refusals do not change the link.**
   - Engine refusals arrive as HTTP 200 with `{ state: "failed", why }`:
     rule, capacity, rate, generation. HTTP admission may instead return
     `{ denied }` with 503; the client validates and preserves that refusal
     without replaying the call or dropping waiting calls.
   - The lane changes the client's link state only on a transport failure:
     no response, or a response that is not this route's JSON.

**Watches need no new API.**
- A query with `ephemeralReads` joins the client's ephemeral interest set
  when it is observed, before its first answer.
- When that set grows, the held `/changes` request is aborted and re-issued
  at once. Shrinking never restarts it.
- A watch is re-run when a `/changes` answer names one of its ephemeral
  tables, and at its `nextExpiry`.
- In the local-first tier, this re-run never triggers a `/sync`, because
  ephemeral changes are not commits.
- §3.3 has the exact parameters and the fencing.

**Expiry recovery (2026-09-27, lane FK).** A local watch can receive a
server answer after its `nextExpiry`, including when the client's estimate
reaches that deadline just before the server does. Withhold that answer and
retry through the ordinary query/publication fences. Keep one request in
flight per watch; repeated expired replies back off at 25, 50, 100 ms and
so on, capped at 1 s, until a current answer arrives or the watch ends.
Without a new wakeup the first expired reply left the watch loading forever;
deadline expiry changes neither the durable sequence nor the ephemeral epoch.
`ephemeral-e2e.test.mjs` now forces the early estimate and keeps its original
empty-result and timing assertions; `expired-server-watch.test.mjs` covers
pacing, withholding, recovery and teardown, with and without `keep`.

The recipes the cards will teach:

```tsx
const typing = useQuery(api.typingIn, { roomId });
const setTyping = useMutation(api.setTyping);
const stopTyping = useMutation(api.stopTyping);
<Composer onChangeText={text => text ? setTyping.run({ roomId }) : stopTyping.run({ roomId })}
          onSend={() => stopTyping.run({ roomId })} />
<TypingIndicator visible={"data" in typing && (typing.data?.length ?? 0) > 0} />
```

The lane compiles this snippet against the real `Read<R>` union and adjusts
the narrowing to whatever the union requires. The card shows the compiled
form.

For presence in a scope ("who has this chat open"), declare
`table viewing … ephemeral 30s` with a `renews` `markViewing` operation. The
card's snippet calls it on mount and every 10 s, and calls a delete
operation on unmount.

### 2.5 Documentation

- A new `ephemeral` guide card, aliased as `typing`. It holds:
  - the typing table, with the membership conjunct on `update`, and its
    three operations;
  - the separate chats-list query, and the sentence "never join an
    ephemeral table into a query that must work offline";
  - the viewing heartbeat, with the React snippet;
  - the refusal table of §2.1;
  - the timing sentence: "a row expires between `0.75 × life` and `life`
    after the last call, plus delivery; for a 5 s bound use `ephemeral 4s`
    or less";
  - the renewal law.
- The `reads` card's typing recipe (`guide/reads.rs:95-130`) becomes a
  pointer to `ephemeral`. The expiring-rows recipe stays for disappearing
  messages, which are durable.
- The `presence` card says: "`connected[p]` is server-wide. For who is in a
  room, typing or viewing, use an ephemeral table (guide ephemeral)."
- `CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md` gets an "Ephemeral writes" section:
  - the lane;
  - coalescing and the renewal window;
  - offline;
  - `Signal<R>`;
  - invalidation and fencing;
  - bounds.
- `SERVER.md` gets the `/changes` `eph` parameters and the rate and capacity
  refusals.
- `packages/snapback4/README.md` gets one line in the orientation. Its
  300-line budget holds.
- `NOT-DOING.md`:
  - line 38 is rewritten to say what remains excluded (§6), with a dated
    Moves entry;
  - nothing leaves the doing-list, because this enacts a standing LLP
    3000.009 §12 line.
- LLP 3000 §7 gets a dated decision, and LLP 3000.009 §12's row is marked
  enacted.
- A `JOURNAL.md` entry records the landing.

## 3. Semantics

### 3.1 Time

- Deadlines use the server clock (`Shared::now`). That is the development
  clock under `dev`, so `dev clock advance` expires ephemeral rows as it
  expires `expire at` rows.
- A row is absent at `now >= deadline`, through the same `expired` check the
  runner already applies to expiring tables. The engine gives ephemeral
  tables a hidden `$deadline` column and an `$expiry` index, like
  `through`'s hidden deadline (`schema/through.rs`).
- **`$deadline` never reaches the wire.**
  - `recorded.rs` `public_image` returns a row unchanged when the table has
    no recorded fields, no `through` and no timeline slots. Ordinary
    ephemeral tables have exactly that shape. That fast path must also
    check for engine fields.
  - `value/disclosed.rs` `Disclosed::visible` must hide `$deadline` as it
    hides recorded fields.
  - Wire tests cover unshaped point reads, scans, and rows returned by
    mutations.
- Generated row types omit engine fields (`emit.rs:24`).

### 3.2 Authorization and revocation

- Ephemeral rows are read under their table's read rule, like any row.
- Rules are evaluated at read time, and ephemeral rows are never cached on a
  device. So a principal who loses membership sees nothing on their next
  read of that room.
- A durable commit to a table a watched query probes invalidates that
  query, as today.
- By the renewal law (§2.2), a departed member's rows outlive the departure
  by at most one life, whatever the update rule says.
- **Disclosure.** The `/changes` answer names ephemeral tables that were
  written. That is the same class as today's table-granular durable
  invalidation, which names every table a commit touched (`serve_changes.rs:80-94`).
  It adds no new leak class. Capacity refusals depend only on the writer's
  own rows (§3.5), except for the global backstop, which reveals only a
  server-wide aggregate, like HTTP admission.

### 3.3 Change notification

**Server state.**
- The memory store keeps:
  - an **epoch**, a `u64` that each committed ephemeral mutation
    increments;
  - an **incarnation**, a random id that changes whenever the store is
    cleared (§3.4);
  - a bounded ring of `(epoch, tables written)`.
- Deadline expiry does not bump the epoch.
  - For a **complete** answer this is safe. No rule probes an ephemeral table
    (§2.1), so expiry can change the answer only by removing rows the answer
    read. Those rows' deadlines are in its `nextExpiry`, and clients
    re-fetch at it.
  - An **incomplete** answer (`complete: false`, for example a scan cut by
    the authorization walk allowance behind hidden rows) can become more
    complete when hidden rows expire, and their deadlines cannot be
    exported. So a watched query with `ephemeralReads` whose latest answer
    is incomplete is re-fetched on a fixed, content-independent 1 s cadence
    while it stays incomplete. (Revision 3: Astra's delta probe put 8,192
    hidden rows ahead of a live row.)
  - The sweep only reclaims memory.

**Wake state, and no lost wakeups.**
- Polls get their own wake state: `Shared.wake: Mutex<Wake { seq, store_identity, generation, eph_incarnation, eph_epoch, ring_tail }>`,
  with a `polls: Condvar`.
- A durable commit updates `seq` under that mutex and notifies `polls`. It
  also notifies `committed` as today, for maintenance.
- An ephemeral commit publishes `(incarnation, epoch, ring tail)` into the
  wake state **while still holding the hub**, taking the wake mutex last
  (hub, then wake), and notifies `polls` only. `clear_and_rebind` publishes
  its new incarnation and hub binding (store identity, generation) together
  under that mutex. Wake publication therefore follows
  commit order. (Revision 3: publishing after releasing the hub let a paused
  commit 1 overwrite commit 2's wake. Astra's protocol model reproduced it.)
- A poll reads its wake snapshot under the wake mutex before `wait_timeout`,
  and re-reads it under the same mutex after a wake.
- On a wake where `seq` is unchanged, a poll takes no engine lock. Only a
  moved `seq` takes the engine for the store check, as today.
- Lock order is: engine mutex, then hub, then wake mutex. Nothing takes the
  hub or the engine while holding the wake mutex. Nothing takes the engine
  while holding the hub.

**`GET /changes` parameters.**
- `eph=<epoch>`.
- `ephi=<incarnation>`.
- `ephTables=<comma-separated table names>`:
  - absent means "wake me for every ephemeral commit";
  - present, possibly empty, means "wake me only for these tables".
- Clients send all three when the backend declares an ephemeral table.
  A client with no ephemeral interest sends `ephTables=` (empty). A
  durable-only backend keeps the old wire: clients omit all three, with no
  initial incarnation reset (§3.6).
- An online client without schema facts learns support from
  `X-Snapback-Ephemeral: 1` on ordinary responses/heartbeats, present only for
  ephemeral backends and exposed through CORS. It loads `/schema` and reissues
  any legacy discovery poll immediately. Generated read facts can enable the
  parameters before discovery. This preserves the entire durable-only request
  flow, including clients whose transport has no `/schema` endpoint.

**When the poll answers.** Exactly as today, plus two conditions:
- the store's sequence moved past `since`;
- the store identity changed;
- the generation differs from the generation at poll entry;
- the wait expired;
- a table in its interest was written after `eph` (new);
- the incarnation differs from `ephi` (new).

**Wake filtering and pacing.**
- A wake caused only by an ephemeral write outside the interest keeps
  waiting. It neither answers nor restarts the heartbeat.
- If the ring no longer covers `eph`, the answer names every table in the
  interest.
- An answer caused only by ephemeral writes is not sent sooner than 100 ms
  after the poll began. That bounds each client to 10 ephemeral-only
  re-polls a second, however fast a session writes.

**What the answer carries.**
- It gains `"ephemeral": { "epoch": n, "incarnation": "…", "tables": [...] }`.
- A poll with none of the three parameters answers exactly as before (the
  field omitted), apart from waking for ephemeral commits. A schema with no
  ephemeral table has none, so for it this is byte-identical to today.

**Re-issuing a poll.**
- Backend adoption reconciles every mounted watch against the adopted
  operation facts, including additions and removals. The client re-issues its
  held poll whenever its ephemeral interest set grows (§2.4).
- The re-issued poll's `eph` is the smallest epoch among the current
  incarnation's latest answers of its ephemeral watches.
- A watch with no answer yet contributes the client's last `/changes`
  epoch.
- A write that lands between an answer and the new poll is therefore always
  after `eph`, and the new poll answers at once.

**Query answers.**
- A query answer that read an ephemeral table carries
  `"ephemeral": { "epoch": n, "incarnation": "…" }`.
- The epoch is read under the hub lock, in the same critical section as the
  memory rows.

**Fencing.**
- The client keeps an **ephemeral revision**, a counter it tags onto every
  ephemeral query request and `/changes` poll it sends.
- Accepting a new incarnation advances the revision. The client then
  invalidates its outstanding ephemeral queries and its poll, and re-issues
  them.
- A response tagged with an older revision is dropped before its
  incarnation is looked at. So `A:50 → B:1 → delayed A:50` cannot reinstate
  the retired incarnation A. (Revision 3: under revision 2's rule, a
  different incarnation was always accepted. Astra's protocol model
  reproduced the resurrection.)
- Within the current revision, each watch remembers its own
  `(incarnation, epoch)` floor.
- An answer from a different incarnation is accepted, advances the
  revision, and replaces the floor.
- An answer from the same incarnation is accepted only if its epoch is at
  least the floor.
- This is independent of durable `seq` fencing (`minimumReadSeq`), and the
  two are never folded together.
- It applies in the online tier's `currentRead`, in `local-query.ts`'s
  publication path, and to `/changes` answers before they are judged stale.

**Presence re-polls.** Poll entry today runs `presence_reconnected`, which
opens an IMMEDIATE transaction (`engine/effects.rs:287-291`). With this
change, it first checks without a write transaction whether the principal
has a pending presence deferral. It opens the write transaction only if it
has one. The effect semantics are unchanged; ephemeral re-polls stop paying
a writer transaction each.

### 3.4 Generation fence, restart, adoption

The memory store is bound to the `(store identity, generation)` it serves.

- Every hub access runs under the hub lock, for mutations and for queries.
  It compares the durable snapshot's `(store identity, generation)`, which
  its read transaction has already established, with the hub's binding:
  - **hub older:** clear it, rebind to the snapshot's pair, and change the
    incarnation;
  - **snapshot older:** refuse with the store's retryable generation
    refusal. The request retries on a fresh reader.
- Adoption (`serve_adoption.rs`) holds the engine mutex. In-place activation
  checks the hub for poison, releases it throughout the SQLite transaction,
  then takes it to clear and rebind after commit. It never holds the hub while
  waiting for SQLite readers: those readers take SQLite before the hub, and a
  DELETE-mode writer needs them to finish before commit or spill. A hub poisoned
  after the initial check is completely reset at committed rebind, avoiding a
  post-commit refusal (LLP 3000.033 §1.3). Engine replacement (`open_engine_at`)
  likewise clears and rebinds the shared hub before publishing the new engine.
- A reader loaned before adoption can therefore never write under the old
  generation's indexes into the new store, nor read new rows under old
  rules.

Ephemeral rows do not survive any of these:
- a server restart;
- schema adoption under `dev`;
- a generation change;
- a store replacement.

Each changes the incarnation, so every client watching an ephemeral table
re-runs its queries (§3.3 fencing).

Day-two classification (`successor.rs`):
- **Destructive:** a durable table gaining `ephemeral`. Activation deletes
  its stored rows and index entries even though the table name remains
  (`store/activation.rs` today deletes only disappeared names).
- **Additive:**
  - an ephemeral table becoming durable, as a new, empty table;
  - adding an ephemeral table;
  - removing one;
  - changing one.

The ephemeral case must be decided before the generic "removed; its rows
are lost" branch (`successor.rs:65-69`). The `$deadline` engine-field
branch is not the only step.

### 3.5 Bounds

| Bound | Value | Refusal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Life | 1 s – 60 s | `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL` at `check`; generation admission re-checks |
| Live rows written by one principal | 1,000 | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Declared bytes written by one principal | 4 MiB | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Live rows, all principals (backstop) | 100,000 | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Declared bytes, all principals (backstop) | 512 MiB | `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` |
| Ephemeral mutations per session | 20 per second sustained, burst 40 | `E_RATE_LIMIT` |
| Waiting calls in a client's lane | 256 | `E_RATE_LIMIT` (local) |
| Rows per group | the table's `cap … by …` | the existing cap refusal |
| Work per mutation and per query | the existing meter | unchanged |

Every refusal in this table is retryable, carries no row detail, and
arrives as HTTP 200 `{ state: "failed", why }` (§2.4 rule 5).

**How capacity is counted.**
- The hub records each row's writing principal.
- A row is charged its **declared maximum image size**, computed from the
  schema's bounds for its columns and index keys, never its actual bytes.
- Per-principal admission therefore depends only on the writer's own rows
  and on the schema. Another principal's private payload cannot flip it
  (Astra F3).
- The global backstop reveals only a server-wide aggregate. That is the
  same residual class as HTTP admission being full.

**When capacity is checked.**
- Capacity counts live rows only. The hub sweeps expired rows before it
  refuses.
- The check runs under the hub lock, after the program and before the epoch
  bump. A refusal undoes the journal: the row is absent and the epoch does
  not move.

**The rate bucket is new.**
- It is a new per-session token bucket, checked before the mutation runs.
- It applies under `dev` and `serve` alike.
- It shares nothing with the login and upload counters that `E_RATE_LIMIT`
  names today. The code is reused, its card text is extended, and its
  family stays `auth`.

The lane's coalescing and renewal window keep an ordinary typing indicator
near one request per second per typist. That is far below these bounds.

### 3.6 What does not change, and what the lock claims are

**What does not change.**
- A schema with no ephemeral table serializes byte-identically. Its wire,
  its device and the published-0.2.33 capability fences are unchanged.
- A new capability name, `ephemeral`, makes an older device refuse a schema
  that has one, instead of queuing its writes.
- `connected[p]` keeps its meaning and its grace.
- Durable mutations, their prediction, the outbox, replay and the partition
  log are unchanged.
- Presence-guarded effects are unchanged.

**The lock claims.** An ephemeral mutation:
- never opens a SQLite write transaction;
- never holds the serving engine's mutex while it executes;
- takes that mutex only for the short sections a query takes: session
  resolution and reader checkout.

Only queries and mutations whose static read closure includes an ephemeral
table take the hub lock, and only for their metered run. Message pages and
other durable queries never wait on it. `MemoryRows` is `!Sync`, so this is
a `Mutex`, not an `RwLock`; the programs that take it are small.

## 4. Implementation

The work runs in two codex `gpt-6-astra` xhigh lanes on the Apartment M5 Pro
mini, from `origin/main` at launch:

- **E1** — compiler, core, engine, server, device guard, guide cards;
  branch `sb4/eph-engine`.
- **E2** — the TypeScript client; branch `sb4/eph-client`, started from
  E1's server checkpoint so that its tests run the real CLI.

File references are from main `71f77c16ca`. The lanes re-verify each
citation.

### 4.1 Language (`crates/snapback4-lang`)

- `ast.rs` `TableDecl` (327-344): `ephemeral: Option<EphemeralDecl { life_ms, site }>`.
- `parser.rs` table body (188-313): add an `ephemeral` arm beside `expire`
  (236-251) and `online` (252-260), reusing `parser/views.rs` `duration_unit`.
- `bind/schema.rs`:
  - `bind_ephemeral` beside `bind_expire` (236-245, 270-283);
  - the refusals of §2.1 in `bind_table` (133-265), `probe` (514) for
    every table, `bind_sync` (285+) and reference columns;
  - the hidden `$deadline` column and `$expiry` index, mirroring
    `bind/through.rs` (218-248).
- A new exhaustive effect visitor (for example `bind/effects.rs`, shared
  with `engine/read_only.rs` if that fits):
  - it classifies programs and computes `renews` and `ephemeralReads`;
  - it drives §2.2's refusals from `bind/mod.rs` (122-140);
  - it sets `ephemeral`, `renews`, `renewMs` and `predicts = "never"` on
    mutation facts, and `ephemeralReads` on query facts;
  - it also produces the `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER` note.
- `bind/residency.rs` (`site` 181-202, `table_line` 494-503,
  `operation_lines` 505-535): the ephemeral residency line.
- `emit.rs` (61-66, 98-104; the import at 19): the facts of §2.4, and the
  `Ephemeral` import when used.
- `guide.rs` `KEYWORDS` (57): the `ephemeral` card and the `typing` alias.
- `guide/codes.rs`:
  - add `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`, `E_EPHEMERAL_FULL` and `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER`;
  - extend `E_RATE_LIMIT`'s text to ephemeral writes;
  - amend `E_OFFLINE` (193).
- `guide/reads.rs` (95-130), `guide/presence.rs` and `tests/guide.rs`:
  rewrite `w10b_typing_card_compiles_and_expires_at_the_deadline`
  (229-244) for the ephemeral recipe, and keep a durable `expire at` test.

### 4.2 Core schema (`crates/snapback4-core`)

- `schema.rs` `Table` (217-259): `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] ephemeral: Option<Ephemeral { life_ms: u64 }>`.
  Use no `untagged` or `flatten`, because the wasm family census refuses
  them.
- `Execution` (53-80): `ephemeral`, `renews` and `renew_ms` on mutations,
  `ephemeral_reads` on queries. Each has a serde default and is skipped when
  false or empty. The wire names are camelCase, as the other facts'
  are.
- `schema/through.rs` (26-45): `is_expiring`, `expiry_column` and
  `engine_field` include ephemeral tables, whose deadline is `$deadline`.
- `schema.rs` `expired` / `expiry_index` (305-318) follow.
- `engine/recorded.rs` (20-23) and `value/disclosed.rs` (152-159, 219-233):
  hide `$deadline` (§3.1).
- `schema/capabilities.rs` (37-49): `("ephemeral", |s| s.tables.values().any(|t| t.ephemeral.is_some()))`.
- Generation admission: re-check the life bound and §2.1's refusals, so a
  hand-edited schema cannot bypass the compiler.
- `successor.rs` (65-135) and `store/activation.rs` (116-125): §3.4.

### 4.3 Engine

A new `engine/ephemeral.rs` (gated on the `sqlite` feature, like the server
engine) adds three things.

- **`Hub`** is shared by `Arc` between the serving engine and its readers.
  It holds:
  - `Mutex<HubState>`, containing:
    - `MemoryRows` (`rows.rs:282-425`);
    - the `(store identity, generation)` binding;
    - per-row writer and declared size;
    - per-principal and global counters;
    - `epoch`, `incarnation` and the ring;
  - `sweep(now)`, which reclaims memory;
  - `clear_and_rebind(identity, generation)`, which also changes the
    incarnation.
- **`Layered<'a> { durable: &dyn Rows, eph: &MemoryRows, journal, mode }`**
  implements `Rows` by routing on the table name.
  - Ephemeral tables go to `eph`, and every mutation of `eph` is
    journaled.
  - Everything else goes to `durable`: durable tables, sequences,
    `effect_once`, assets and prefetch.
  - In ephemeral-mutation mode, any durable write is an internal refusal.
  - A drop guard undoes the journal unless the commit completed (§2.2).
- **Queries.** Only a query whose static `ephemeralReads` is non-empty runs
  over `Layered` and takes the hub lock. It holds the lock for its metered
  run. All other queries run exactly as today.

**`mutate_ephemeral`.** `Engine::mutate` checks the operation's `ephemeral`
fact right after its kind checks (`engine/mod.rs` 946-1058). It then
branches to `mutate_ephemeral`, which:

1. Opens a **read** transaction on the durable store (`read_transaction`,
   deferred), never `transaction()`. A pooled reader engine does this, and
   the serving engine's mutex is not held while it runs.
2. Locks the hub and applies §3.4's generation check.
3. Runs the program over `Layered`, stamping `$deadline` and applying the
   renewal law in `write.rs`:
   - at insert, after defaults (58-62, before validation at 127);
   - at update, as its own assignment beside `recompute_deadline` (281).
4. On success, checks §3.5's capacity (undoing and refusing if exceeded),
   bumps the epoch and records the written tables. On any other exit, the
   drop guard undoes the journal.
5. Publishes the wake while still holding the hub (hub, then wake mutex;
   §3.3), then releases the hub.
6. Writes nothing to `_commits`, `_log`, `_outbox` or `_ops`.

It returns the result, the epoch, the incarnation and the written tables.

**Sweeps.** `sweep_expired_for` (`engine/mod.rs:780-820`) skips ephemeral
tables. The hub's `sweep` runs from the maintenance loop.

**Other paths.**
- `seed_at`, `load` and `/seed` refuse ephemeral tables.
- `snapback4 test` uses the in-process hub, so `test` can exercise the
  typing recipe.
- `snapback4 run` of an ephemeral mutation prints a note: the row lives in
  this process only, and a running `dev` server's clients will not see it.
- The Postgres store's `supported()` (`postgres/mod.rs:396-421`) refuses a
  schema with an ephemeral table until increment C3.

### 4.4 Server (`crates/snapback4`)

- `serve.rs` `/m/` (930-1087):
  1. Before the replay lookup (947), an ephemeral operation checks the rate
     bucket.
  2. It takes a pooled reader (as `/q/` does at 995-1015), with the same
     in-memory fallback to the serving engine under `--fresh`
     (`serve.rs:1010-1013`), and calls `mutate_ephemeral`.
  3. It replies HTTP 200 with
     `{ "state": "sent", "id": <id|null>, "result": <json>, "ephemeral": { "epoch", "incarnation", "tables" }, "store_id", "head" }`
     and no `seq`. A refusal replies HTTP 200
     `{ "state": "failed", "id", "why", "store_id", "head" }`.
- `/q/` answers of queries with `ephemeralReads` carry
  `"ephemeral": { "epoch", "incarnation" }` (§3.3).
- `Shared.wake` and `polls` (§3.3). The durable commit path at 1075-1079
  updates `wake.seq` and notifies `polls` as well as `committed`.
- `serve_changes.rs` (29-55):
  - §3.3's parameters, snapshot-under-mutex waiting, wake filtering,
    100 ms pacing and the answer field;
  - `presence_reconnected`'s read-first check.
- `serve_maintenance.rs` (116-137): the hub sweep on every pass.
- `serve_adoption.rs` (157-190) and engine replacement: hub
  `clear_and_rebind` under the engine mutex.
- `verbs.rs`: `data` and `sql` skip ephemeral tables and say why.

### 4.5 Device (`crates/snapback4-device`)

- `admit_with_replay` (`device.rs:952`) refuses an ephemeral operation with
  `E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL`, as a guard. The client never sends one there.
- Reads of an ephemeral table are already `unknown`, because the table has
  no `sync` (`require_acquired`, `device.rs:320-333`).
- The wasm budget (`metrics/wasm-budget.json`: 578,207 B, against 577,207 B
  measured on the black M5 and 576,963 B on the mini): if the device's wasm
  exceeds it, the lane raises the budget in the same commit and records
  the reason.

### 4.6 Client (`packages/snapback4`)

- `contract.ts`: `Mutation`/`Ephemeral`/`Signal` as in §2.4, and the
  `mutate` overloads. `backend.ts`: the new operation facts.
- A new `src/ephemeral.ts` implements the lane (§2.4):
  - order;
  - coalescing and the window for `renews` operations;
  - offline;
  - the 256 bound;
  - refusals that do not change the link;
  - `POST /m/<name>` with a correlation id and no `MutationIds`;
  - no `onWrite` or outbox bookkeeping.
- Branch on `op.ephemeral` at the top of every `mutate`:
  - `client.ts` (658-685);
  - `local.ts` (1152-1205);
  - `app-online.ts` (105-150);
  - `app-client.ts` (82-106);
  - `app.ts` (201, 346);
  - `mock.ts`, where the labelled mock keeps ephemeral rows in memory with
    the same life semantics.
- Interest and polling:
  - `changes.ts` (48): the three parameters.
  - Both poll loops (`client.ts:505-537` and `local.ts:806-845`):
    - keep the interest set from `ephemeralReads`;
    - abort and re-issue on growth, with §3.3's `eph`;
    - re-run intersecting watches on an answer's `ephemeral.tables`,
      without counting it as a change and without `/sync`;
    - re-run every ephemeral watch on an incarnation change;
    - keep polling while any watch reads an ephemeral table.
- Fencing (§3.3): the per-watch `(incarnation, epoch)` floor, in
  `client.ts` `currentRead` (413) and in the `local-query.ts` publication
  path.
- `react.tsx`: `useMutation`'s `run` follows the overloads; `usePendingWrite`
  takes only `Mutation`.

### 4.7 Tests

Each of the following becomes a test the suite runs.

**Language** (`crates/snapback4-lang/tests`):
- Every refusal in §2.1 and §2.2, with its code, message and fix text:
  - a rule on an ephemeral table probing another ephemeral table;
  - a mixed write hidden inside an expression block (IR probe);
  - a mixed write through a helper.
- The classification, `renews` (positive and negative cases, including the
  `n: old.n + 1` upsert keyed by `viewer`, a toggle and a returning
  operation) and `ephemeralReads`.
- `N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER`.
- The emitted facts.
- A schema with no ephemeral table emits and serializes byte-identically,
  compared with fixtures captured at the base commit before any change.

**Guide:** the `ephemeral` card compiles and runs:
- typing set, read by a peer, expired at `life`;
- the chats-list query;
- viewing presence.

**Core:**
- `Layered` routing.
- Durable-write refusal in ephemeral mode.
- The drop-guard undo on a refusal after a successful earlier write, for
  each of these: a unique clash, a cap, a rule, a store error.
- The renewal law: a member removed at t=1100 is refused at the next
  renewal, and their row is gone by t = 1100 + life. This is the reviewers'
  probe, inverted.
- Joins from an ephemeral table to durable tables under their rules.
- Expiry before rules.
- `$deadline` absent from point reads, scans and mutation-returned rows.
- Per-principal and global capacity refusals: the row is absent and the
  epoch unmoved.
- A **paired-world capacity test**: two worlds with equal topology and
  different private payload lengths give identical admission.
- The generation fence: a reader whose transaction predates adoption is
  refused, and the hub is rebound.
- Nothing is written to `_commits`, `_log`, `_outbox` or `_ops` (assert
  row counts before and after).
- Wake order: a publisher paused between its commit and its wake
  publication, across another commit and across adoption, never leaves the
  wake state behind the hub's epoch or incarnation.

**Server** (a new `tests/serve_ephemeral` module beside `serve_presence`):
- An ephemeral mutation succeeds while another connection holds a SQLite
  write transaction.
- A peer's poll **already blocked inside `wait_timeout`** with
  `ephTables=typing` answers within 250 ms of a `setTyping`, and names
  `typing`. This is the lost-wakeup test.
- A poll with `ephTables=viewing` does not answer on a write to `typing`.
- A poll with no `eph` parameters on a schema with no ephemeral tables
  answers byte-identically to the base.
- The 100 ms pacing.
- The rate refusal, as HTTP 200 `failed`.
- Restart and adoption change the incarnation.
- A removed member's next read is empty.

**Client:**
- The lane:
  - order: a delayed `set → set → stop` settles as stopped;
  - `renews` coalescing, where the replaced promise resolves with its
    replacement's outcome;
  - non-`renews` calls are never coalesced or suppressed;
  - the renewal window at call time and at dequeue, including that an
    intervening `stopTyping` closes it;
  - the 256 bound.
- An offline call, and every call waiting when the link drops, resolves at
  once with `E_OFFLINE`, and leaves nothing in the outbox or journal.
- A capacity or rate refusal leaves the link `online`.
- A poll already held before a watch with `ephemeralReads` mounts is
  re-issued, and the watch sees a later write within 500 ms. This is run on
  the local-first tier, which is the benchmark's tier, and on the online
  tier.
- The `(incarnation, epoch)` fencing and the ephemeral revision:
  - a stale same-incarnation answer is dropped;
  - a new-incarnation epoch-1 answer is accepted;
  - delayed `/q/` and `/changes` responses from the retired incarnation,
    arriving after the replacement, are dropped.
- An incomplete ephemeral answer is re-fetched at the 1 s cadence, and picks
  up a row made reachable by silent reclamation, without either
  notification counter changing.
- The `nextExpiry` re-fetch.
- In the local-first tier, an ephemeral invalidation re-runs the watch
  without `/sync`.
- Type tests:
  - `mutate(api.eph)` is `Signal<R>`;
  - widening `Ephemeral` to `Mutation` is a type error;
  - `usePendingWrite(api.eph)` is a type error;
  - the card's React snippet compiles.

**End-to-end** (`packages/snapback4/test/ephemeral-e2e.test.mjs`, a real CLI
server, two clients on the local-first tier). This is the acceptance test:
1. Client B mounts `typingIn` after its poll is already held.
2. Client A calls `setTyping` 45 times at 25 ms intervals, as the benchmark
   does.
3. The test records when B first sees A and when B stops seeing A.
4. Over five trials:
   - first-seen must be at most 500 ms after A's first call;
   - last-seen must be at most `life + 500 ms` after A's last call;
   - with `ephemeral 4s`.
5. A durable-typing control (the old recipe) runs beside it. Its numbers
   are recorded, not asserted, so the change is measured, not assumed.

**Suite:** `scripts/test.sh` stays green, and the wasm build stays within
budget.

### 4.8 Sequence and publication

1. Revision 1 landed on main as Draft. It then had three blind reviews
   (§8): Astra `gpt-6-astra` at max effort, Grok 4.7 at xhigh, and Opus 5.5
   at max effort.
2. Revision 2 folds all three reviews. Astra, the family that was NOT
   READY, gets one bounded delta check of revision 2, which runs in
   parallel with lane E1. Anything it finds is folded into the lanes by a
   continuation brief. There is no further design round.
3. E1, then E2 from E1's server checkpoint.
4. Two blind code reviews of the integrated branch, by Astra and Grok, with
   at most two fix rounds.
5. Land on main.
6. Publish the next free `snapback4` version with `scripts/publish.mjs`,
   plus `snapback4-expo`.
7. Run a Clique calibration with Astra medium on the published pin, and
   record it under `benchmarks/runs/`.

+fn ephemeral_operation(bound:&Bound,p:&Program,out:&mut Campaign)->bool{
+    let unsupported=p.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&bound.schema.operations.get(&p.name).is_some_and(|f|f.ephemeral);

+// Authored seed/fixture data is an explicit trusted initial assumption. A raw
+// row-read admission witness is failing; column privacy still needs an actual
+// authored observer and synthetic mutations remain supplemental.
+fn trusted_reads(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
+    let Ok(rows)=readback(fixture)else{return;};
+    let Ok(mut judge)=fixture.fork_fixture()else{return;};
+    let actors=histories::actors(bound,&rows);
+    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.is_none()){
+        if inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(|policy|kernel::implies(&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].rules.read,policy)==Ok(true)){continue;}
+        if rows.get(&inv.table).is_some_and(|rs|rs.len()>1000){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: raw authored fixture admission samples the first 1000 rows; remaining rows uncovered",inv.target_label()));}
+        'rows:for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().take(1000){for viewer in &actors{
+            if !out.reserve(1){return;}
+            if !live(&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table],row)||!matches!(judge.fixture_policy(&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].rules.read,viewer,None,row,NOW),Ok(rules::Verdict::Readable))||policy_in(bound,inv,&rows,viewer,None,row,&mut judge)!=Ok(false){continue;}
+            out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace,invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:BTreeMap::new(),viewer:viewer.clone(),operation:"$fixture.read-admission".into(),effect:"read".into(),row:row.clone(),old:None,supporting_rows:rows.clone(),rule_path:"ordinary row-read gate admits authored fixture data contrary to declared intent".into(),scope:"authored fixture initialization is trusted; raw row admission, no initialization history claimed".into(),observations:None,history:Vec::new()});
+            break 'rows;
+        }}

+// Counterfactual field mutation must not break an authored creator's copies
+// or recipient guards. Direct typed payload observations run independently.
+fn coupled(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->bool{
+    let column=inv.column.as_deref().unwrap();
+    let table=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
+    if table.rules.insert.needs_field_value(column,&table.columns[column])||table.update_rule_judged().needs_field_value(column,&table.columns[column]){return true;}
+    fn definitions(value:&serde_json::Value,out:&mut BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>){
+        if let Some(object)=value.as_object(){
+            for tag in ["Const","Let","Assign"]{if let Some(binding)=object.get(tag)&&let (Some(name),Some(value))=(binding.get("name").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str),binding.get("value")){
+                if tag=="Assign"||out.contains_key(name){out.insert(name.into(),serde_json::Value::Null);}else{out.insert(name.into(),value.clone());}
+            }}
+            for child in object.values(){definitions(child,out);}
+        }else if let Some(xs)=value.as_array(){for child in xs{definitions(child,out);}}
+    }
+    fn fields<'a>(expression:&'a serde_json::Value,bindings:&'a BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>,depth:usize)->Option<&'a serde_json::Map<String,serde_json::Value>>{
+        if depth>32{return None;}
+        if let Some(map)=expression.get("Object").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object){return Some(map);}
+        if let Some(projection)=expression.get("Builtin")&&projection.get("builtin").is_some_and(|b|b.get("ProjectFields").is_some()){
+            return fields(projection.get("args")?.as_array()?.first()?,bindings,depth+1);
+        }
+        if let Some(name)=expression.get("Variable").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str){return fields(bindings.get(name)?,bindings,depth+1);}
+        None
+    }
+    fn dependency(value:&serde_json::Value,arg:&str,table:&str,args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,bindings:&BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>,depth:usize)->bool{
+        if depth>32{return true;}
+        if let Some(field)=value.get("Field")&&let (Some(base),Some(name))=(field.get("base"),field.get("name").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str))&&let Some(map)=fields(base,bindings,depth+1){
+            return map.get(name).is_none_or(|expression|dependency(expression,arg,table,args,bindings,depth+1));
+        }
+        if let Some(name)=value.get("Variable").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str){
+            if name==arg{return true;}
+            if let Some(bound)=bindings.get(name){return bound.is_null()||dependency(bound,arg,table,args,bindings,depth+1);}
+            return !args.contains_key(name);
+        }
+        // A guard re-reading the written table may inspect the supplied field
+        // through a row alias even when the argument itself occurs only once.
+        for tag in ["Get","Exists"]{if value.get(tag).and_then(|v|v.get("table")).and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some(table){return true;}}
+        match value{serde_json::Value::Object(xs)=>xs.values().any(|v|dependency(v,arg,table,args,bindings,depth+1)),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|v|dependency(v,arg,table,args,bindings,depth+1)),_=>false}
+    }
+    fn guarded(value:&serde_json::Value,arg:&str,table:&str,args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,bindings:&BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>)->bool{
+        for tag in ["Require","Check","If"]{if let Some(condition)=value.get(tag).and_then(|v|v.get("condition"))&&dependency(condition,arg,table,args,bindings,0){return true;}}
+        for tag in ["Binary","Unary","Builtin","Emit","JobAs","JobQuery"]{if let Some(expression)=value.get(tag){
+            if tag=="Builtin"&&expression.get("builtin").is_some_and(|b|b.get("ProjectFields").is_some()){continue;}
+            if dependency(expression,arg,table,args,bindings,0){return true;}
+        }}
+        match value{serde_json::Value::Object(xs)=>xs.values().any(|v|guarded(v,arg,table,args,bindings)),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|v|guarded(v,arg,table,args,bindings)),_=>false}
+    }
+    fn related_write(value:&serde_json::Value,arg:&str,table:&str,column:&str,args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,bindings:&BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>)->bool{
+        for tag in ["Insert","InsertIfAbsent","Upsert","Update","WriteTarget"]{
+            let Some(write)=value.get(tag)else{continue;};
+            let name=write.get("table").or_else(||write.get("target").and_then(|t|t.get("table"))).and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str);
+            if let Some(row)=write.get("row").or_else(||write.get("patch")){
+                if let Some(map)=fields(row,bindings,0){
+                    if map.iter().any(|(field,expression)|(name!=Some(table)||field!=column)&&dependency(expression,arg,table,args,bindings,0)){return true;}
+                }else if dependency(row,arg,table,args,bindings,0){return true;}
+            }
+            if write.get("key").or_else(||write.get("target").and_then(|target|target.get("key"))).is_some_and(|key|dependency(key,arg,table,args,bindings,0))||write.get("condition").is_some_and(|condition|dependency(condition,arg,table,args,bindings,0)){return true;}
+        }
+        match value{serde_json::Value::Object(xs)=>xs.values().any(|v|related_write(v,arg,table,column,args,bindings)),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|v|related_write(v,arg,table,column,args,bindings)),_=>false}
+    }
+    fn writes(value:&serde_json::Value,table:&str,column:&str,args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,program:&str,bindings:&BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>,guards:&BTreeSet<String>,defaulted:bool,found:&mut bool,coupled:&mut bool){
+        if let Some(object)=value.as_object(){
+            for tag in ["Insert","InsertIfAbsent","Upsert","Update","WriteTarget"]{
+                let Some(write)=object.get(tag)else{continue;};
+                let name=write.get("table").or_else(||write.get("target").and_then(|t|t.get("table"))).and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str);
+                if name!=Some(table){continue;}
+                let map=write.get("row").or_else(||write.get("patch")).and_then(|e|fields(e,bindings,0));
+                let Some(map)=map else{*found=true;*coupled=true;continue;};
+                if let Some(expression)=map.get(column){
+                    *found=true;
+                    if let Some(name)=expression.get("Variable").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)&&args.contains_key(name){
+                        let occurrence=serde_json::to_string(&Expr::Variable(name.into())).unwrap();
+                        if program.matches(&occurrence).count()!=1||guards.contains(name){*coupled=true;}
+                    }else{*coupled=true;}
+                }else if tag=="Upsert"||defaulted&&matches!(tag,"Insert"|"InsertIfAbsent"){*found=true;*coupled=true;}
+            }
+            for child in object.values(){writes(child,table,column,args,program,bindings,guards,defaulted,found,coupled);}
+        }else if let Some(xs)=value.as_array(){for child in xs{writes(child,table,column,args,program,bindings,guards,defaulted,found,coupled);}}
+    }
+    let(mut found,mut unsafe_writer)=(false,false);
+    for p in &bound.programs{let json=serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap();let text=serde_json::to_string(&p.program).unwrap();let mut bindings=BTreeMap::new();definitions(&json,&mut bindings);let guards=p.program.args.keys().filter(|arg|guarded(&json,arg,&inv.table,&p.program.args,&bindings)||related_write(&json,arg,&inv.table,column,&p.program.args,&bindings)).cloned().collect();writes(&json,&inv.table,column,&p.program.args,&text,&bindings,&guards,table.defaults.contains_key(column),&mut found,&mut unsafe_writer);}
+    found&&unsafe_writer
+}

+// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#137-wave-19-correction-admissible-starts-and-persistent-refutations — creator guards/copies are part of generated initialization.
+pub fn fixture(bound:&Bound,out:&mut Campaign)->Result<Engine,String>{
+    let proposed=proposals(bound,out)?;
+    let rows=readback(&proposed)?;
+    let actors=histories::actors(bound,&rows);
+    // A principal key and a field named role are insufficient: payload tables
+    // must execute their creators even when they happen to have that shape.
+    fn authority_roster(bound:&Bound,name:&str)->bool{
+        let columns=super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,name);
+        fn used(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,name:&str,columns:&BTreeSet<String>)->bool{match rule{
+            RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..} if table==name&&key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,v)|v)).any(|v|matches!(v,RuleTerm::Viewer))=>super::super::privilege::probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities).iter().any(|(c,v)|columns.contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(_)))),
+            RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|r|used(bound,r,name,columns)),RuleExpr::Not(r)=>used(bound,r,name,columns),_=>false,
+        }}
+        !columns.is_empty()&&bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|used(bound,r,name,&columns)))
+    }
+    let rosters=bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name,_)|authority_roster(bound,name)).map(|(name,_)|name.clone()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
+    let creators=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&!p.program.native&&!bound.schema.operations.get(&p.program.name).is_some_and(|f|f.ephemeral)&&ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(_,kind)|matches!(*kind,"insert"|"upsert"))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+    let created=creators.iter().flat_map(|p|ir::write_tables(&p.program)).filter(|(_,kind)|matches!(*kind,"insert"|"upsert")).map(|(t,_)|t).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
+    let mut engine=engine_on_store(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new(),proposed.store().empty_with_asset_ledger().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?)?;
+    let mut pending=rows.iter().filter(|(t,_)|rosters.contains(*t)||!created.contains(*t)).flat_map(|(t,rs)|rs.iter().map(move|r|(t.clone(),r.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+    // Bootstrap proposals require an actor admitted by the insert rule in the
+    // previous world. Never let a proposed role authorize its own insertion.
+    for _ in 0..=bound.schema.tables.len(){
+        let before=pending.len();let mut retry=Vec::new();
+        for (table,row) in pending{
+            if !out.reserve(1){out.unsupported.insert("generated initialization work exhausted; remaining populations uncovered".into());validate_fixture(bound,&mut engine)?;return Ok(engine);}
+            let gate=&bound.schema.tables[&table].rules.insert;
+            if !actors.iter().any(|actor|matches!(engine.fixture_policy(gate,actor,None,&row,NOW),Ok(rules::Verdict::Readable))) {retry.push((table,row));continue;}
+            let mut candidate=engine.fork_fixture().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
+            let seeded=clean_row(bound,&table,&row);
+            if candidate.seed_at([(table.clone(),seeded)],NOW).is_ok(){engine=candidate;}else{retry.push((table,row));}
+        }
+        pending=retry;if pending.len()==before{break;}
+    }
+    for (table,_) in pending{out.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture {table}: no admitted insert/bootstrap; population uncovered"));}
+    // Authored insertion bodies preserve requires, immutable copies, defaults,
+    // role checks on principal arguments and multi-table creation invariants.
+    let mut attempts=0usize;
+    for _ in 0..=bound.schema.tables.len().min(12){
+        let before=readback(&engine)?;
+        for bp in &creators{
+            let p=&bp.program;let writes=ir::write_tables(p);
+            if writes.iter().all(|(t,_)|rosters.contains(t)){continue;}
+            let current=readback(&engine)?;
+            let targets=writes.iter().filter(|(_,kind)|matches!(*kind,"insert"|"upsert")).map(|(t,_)|t).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+            if targets.iter().all(|t|current.get(*t).map_or(0,Vec::len)>=2){continue;}
+            let cases=histories::fixture_cases(p,&current,&actors,out);
+            'cases:for args in cases.into_iter().take(16){for actor in &actors{
+                if attempts>=2048||!out.reserve(1){out.unsupported.insert("generated authored initialization capped at 2048 calls; remaining creators uncovered".into());validate_fixture(bound,&mut engine)?;return Ok(engine);}
+                attempts+=1;
+                let mut candidate=engine.fork_fixture().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
+                let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut candidate,&p.args,&args,actor){Ok(a)=>a,Err(_)=>continue};
+                let result=candidate.mutate(&p.name,None,Context::new(actor,args).at(NOW));
+                if matches!(result,Ok(r) if matches!(r.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..})) {
+                    let after=readback(&candidate)?;
+                    if targets.iter().any(|t|after.get(*t).map_or(0,Vec::len)>current.get(*t).map_or(0,Vec::len)) {engine=candidate;}
+                    let after=readback(&engine)?;
+                    if targets.iter().all(|t|after.get(*t).map_or(0,Vec::len)>=2){break 'cases;}
+                }
+            }}
+        }
+        if readback(&engine)?==before{break;}
+    }
+    for table in created.difference(&rosters){if engine.store().rows(table).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.is_empty(){out.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture {table}: authored creator did not populate this table"));}}
+    validate_fixture(bound,&mut engine)?;Ok(engine)
+}
+fn clean_row(bound:&Bound,table:&str,row:&Value)->Value{
+    let t=&bound.schema.tables[table];
+    match row{Value::Row(fields)=>Value::Row(fields.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)&&!t.recorded.contains_key(*c)&&!t.maintained.contains_key(*c)).map(|(c,v)|(c.clone(),v.clone())).collect()),_=>row.clone()}
+}

+// Prioritize actually admitted relationship changes rather than filling the

+fn relationship_candidates<'a>(bound:&Bound,p:&'a Program,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],cases:&[BTreeMap<String,Value>],fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign)->Vec<(&'a Program,String,BTreeMap<String,Value>)>{
