## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts and five actionable notes; `--verbose` retains every reason.
A mirror invariant cannot catch a wrong rule: the advisory asks
for the spec's audience, resource relationship and lifecycle condition instead.
A legitimate simple owner policy can be equivalent; the advisory never refuses it.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: it offers unconfirmed
per-role/per-column alternatives and questions, never auto-accepted policy.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (both insert and update). `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

### E_SECURITY_TARGET

E_SECURITY_TARGET means the invariant target does not name a declared table, column or effect. Correct the target name; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SECURITY_INTENT

N_SECURITY_INTENT means access rules lack independent declared intent. Declare policy from the spec; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_BROAD_READ

N_BROAD_READ identifies universal or roster-wide readership; its message states the exact condition. Confirm the intended audience; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE means a stored grant may survive a later role change. Recheck the current reader role; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_ARGUMENT_GRANT

N_ARGUMENT_GRANT means an argument becomes a reader-grant column. Validate the recipient role and relationship; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE

N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE means optional media permits omitted evidence. Declare required media when the spec requires it; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE

N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE means sweeps leave time between a deadline and cleanup. Enforce the deadline in the mutation; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE

N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE means absence and later denial can reveal different outcomes. Unify the code and message when existence is private; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

The isolated production HTTP campaign also signs in, refreshes the session,
logs out all sessions and retries both retired credentials against query,
mutation, schema, changes, sync, probe and refresh doors. It reports missing coverage
when ordinary identity or complete deployed worker artifacts are unavailable.
This checks server admission; a disconnected device can still retain old bytes.

### N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies self-signup roles or role-grant operations that can confer authority trusted by other rules. Restrict assignment to authorized delegation; provision the first administrator with `guide staging`.

Identity rate refusals return HTTP `Retry-After` (seconds) and
`denied.retryAfterMs` (milliseconds), conservatively covering the 60-second
window. Wait that interval before retrying identity requests.

REL5 owns release transitions/old-client compatibility; MIG owns backfills and
staging clock control for day-two qualification.

### N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE

N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE means intent and the effective rule admit the same cases
in the bounded predicate kernel. Check the original requirement before rewriting; an equivalent policy
may be correct, but cannot detect a mistake copied into both declarations.

### N_ROLE_WIDE_READ

N_ROLE_WIDE_READ means a role-wide branch gated by a row value exposes every column
on matching rows.
Use a board/projection table for a public rating; declare intent for the private
notes, principals, images and files separately.

`test` uses generation when fixtures are absent or contain no rows, including
an empty starter fixtures directory. Populated authored fixtures stay selected;
seeded read and integrity violations fail under that explicit starting-state
assumption. Generated payloads run authored creation mutations, preserving
requires, immutable copies and recipient-role checks; declined populations are
coverage gaps. A recorded role change or relationship transfer is shown in the
counterexample history; a stale starting snapshot is not such a history.

A completed `test` records concrete refutations under
`snapback/generated/security-test.json`. The next `check` reports matching
failures even when its short campaign cannot rediscover them. Exact source,
fixture bytes and CLI identity bind the record; edits invalidate it. No cached
passing result proves security. Every escalation note stays visible in short
output. Rule-changing deploy previews print exposure counts and demand a test
for the selected source/fixtures when a matching full finite campaign is absent.
async function snapshot() {
  const count = JSON.parse(await checked(["sql", "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets", "--release", directory, "--json"])).rows[0][0];
  const full = JSON.parse(await checked(["sql", "SELECT id, owner, body, weight, length FROM tickets ORDER BY id", "--release", directory, "--limit", String(Math.max(1, count + 1)), "--json"]));
  assert.equal(full.rows.length, count, "complete population");
  return new Map(full.rows.map(row => [row[0], row]));
}
if (existsSync(directory + "/store.sqlite")) await rollback(directory, 1);
else await deploy(directory, "versions/v1");
const host = await startServe(directory);
try {
  const before = await snapshot();
  const users = [];
  for (const role of ["member", "reviewer"]) {
    const signed = await signup(host.url, `${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, randomUUID() + randomUUID());
    assert.equal(signed.ok, true);
    users.push(signed.session);
  }
  const [alice, reviewer] = users;
  async function post(path, session, args) {
    const response = await fetch(host.url + path, { method:"POST", headers:{"content-type":"application/json", authorization:`Bearer ${session.token}`}, body:JSON.stringify({args}), signal:AbortSignal.timeout(10000) });
    assert.equal(response.status, 200);
    return response.json();
  }
  const operator = {token:await host.operatorToken()};
  for (const [index, role] of ["member", "reviewer"].entries()) {
    assert.equal((await post("/m/provision", operator, {principal:users[index].principal, role})).state, "sent");
  }
  const first = await post("/m/add", alice, {body:"historical ticket", weight:1000, length:400});
  assert.equal(first.state, "sent");
  const ticket = first.result.id;
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data.body, "historical ticket", "v1 reviewer can read");
  const historical = await snapshot(); assert.equal(historical.size, before.size + 1);
  const preview = await checked(["deploy", directory, "--schema", "versions/v2", "--check", "--restore-dormant"]);
  assert.match(preview, /vehicleClass/);
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), historical, "read-only preview");
  await deploy(directory, "versions/v2", {args:["--restore-dormant"]});
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), historical, "historical fields preserved");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "car");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "v2 tightened privacy");
  const oldCall = await post("/m/add", alice, {body:"old-shaped call on v2", weight:4000, length:400});
  assert.equal(oldCall.state, "sent"); assert.equal(oldCall.result.vehicleClass, "commercial");
  const allV2 = await snapshot(); assert.equal(allV2.size, historical.size + 1);
  await rollback(directory, 1);
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), allV2, "native rows preserved");
  const oldView = (await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data;
  assert.equal(oldView.body, "historical ticket"); assert.ok(!("vehicleClass" in oldView));
  assert.equal((await post("/m/revise", alice, {ticket, weight:4000})).state, "sent");
  const changed = await snapshot();
  const expected = new Map(allV2); const changedRow = [...expected.get(ticket)]; changedRow[3] = 4000; expected.set(ticket, changedRow);
  assert.deepEqual(changed, expected, "only intended edit");
  await rollback(directory, "latest");
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), changed, "complete population preserved");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "commercial", "old edit caught up");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "privacy restored");
  console.log(JSON.stringify({status:"passed", persistent:true, before:before.size, after:changed.size, backfill:"car → commercial after v1 edit", privacy:"v1 broad → v2 private → v1 broad → v2 private"}));
} finally { await host.stop(); }
```

Storage, users and rows survive. Resolve reported conflicts through a truthful bridge.

When table rules change, deploy and deploy --check print a short security
exposure summary. Without a matching completed security campaign they demand
`snapback4 test` for the selected source and fixtures (saved-source previews name
`test --source`). Known refutations and coverage gaps remain visible; a finite
campaign is not proof. This reminder does not change activation authority.

/bin/bash -lc "timeout 20s git show 6024498:snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/ephemeral.rs | sed -n '1,165p'; timeout 20s git show 6024498:snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/ephemeral.rs | sed -n '240,365p'; timeout 20s rg -n 'fn fork_memory|fn seed_at|seed_at\\(' snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store* snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine* | head -45; timeout 20s git show 6024498:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write.rs | head -210" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4

//! One process's short-lived rows and commit epoch, shared by every reader.
// @ref LLP 3000.017#4.3 — no SQLite writer, log, replay record or durable receipt.
mod layered;
#[cfg(test)]
mod wake_tests;
use super::*;
use crate::rows::{Bounds, Dir, MemoryRows, Rows};
pub use crate::schema::ephemeral::Version;
use crate::schema::{Schema, Table};
pub use layered::Layered;
use std::{
    collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet, VecDeque},
    sync::{Arc, Mutex, MutexGuard},
};

pub const RING_SIZE: usize = 256;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct Change {
    pub preserved: Vec<String>,
    pub epoch: u64,
    pub incarnation: String,
    pub tables: Vec<String>,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Snapshot {
    pub preserved: VecDeque<(u64, Vec<String>)>,
    pub identity: String,
    pub generation: u64,
    pub version: Version,
    pub ring: VecDeque<(u64, Vec<String>)>,
    pub tables: Vec<String>,
}
impl Snapshot {
    pub fn preserved_since(&self, epoch: u64, incarnation: &str) -> Vec<String> {
        if incarnation != self.version.incarnation || epoch >= self.version.epoch { return vec![]; }
        let mut result: Option<Vec<String>> = None;
        let mut previous = epoch;
        for (at, tables) in self.preserved.iter().filter(|(at,_)| *at > epoch) {
            if *at != previous + 1 { return vec![]; }
            if let Some(result) = &mut result { result.retain(|t| tables.contains(t)); } else { result = Some(tables.clone()); }
            previous = *at;
        }
        if previous != self.version.epoch { return vec![]; }
        result.unwrap_or_default()
    }

    pub fn changed(
        &self,
        epoch: u64,
        incarnation: Option<&str>,
        interest: Option<&BTreeSet<String>>,
    ) -> Vec<String> {
        let allowed = |name: &String| interest.is_none_or(|set| set.contains(name));
        if incarnation.is_some_and(|id| id != self.version.incarnation)
            || epoch > self.version.epoch
            || self
                .ring
                .front()
                .is_some_and(|(first, _)| epoch.saturating_add(1) < *first)
        {
            return match interest {
                Some(tables) => tables.iter().cloned().collect(),
                None => self.tables.clone(),
            };
        }
        self.ring
            .iter()
            .filter(|(at, _)| *at > epoch)
            .flat_map(|(_, tables)| tables.iter())
            .filter(|t| allowed(t))
            .cloned()
            .collect::<BTreeSet<_>>()
            .into_iter()
            .collect()
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Limits {
    pub principal_rows: u64,
    pub principal_bytes: u64,
    pub rows: u64,
    pub bytes: u64,
}
impl Default for Limits {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            principal_rows: 1000,
            principal_bytes: 4 * 1024 * 1024,
            rows: 100_000,
            bytes: 512 * 1024 * 1024,
        }
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default)]
struct Count {
    rows: u64,
    bytes: u64,
}
impl Count {
    fn add(&mut self, bytes: u64) {
        self.rows += 1;
        self.bytes = self.bytes.saturating_add(bytes);
    }
    fn remove(&mut self, bytes: u64) {
        self.rows -= 1;
        self.bytes -= bytes;
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
struct Accounting {
    owners: BTreeMap<(String, String), (String, u64)>,
    principals: BTreeMap<String, Count>,
    total: Count,
}
struct Plan {
    changes: Vec<((String, String), Option<(String, u64)>)>,
    principals: BTreeMap<String, Count>,
    total: Count,
}
impl Accounting {
    fn remove(&mut self, key: &(String, String)) {
        if let Some((principal, bytes)) = self.owners.remove(key) {
            self.total.remove(bytes);
            let count = self.principals.get_mut(&principal).unwrap();
            count.remove(bytes);
            if count.rows == 0 {
                self.principals.remove(&principal);
            }
        }
    }
    fn plan(
        &self,
        layer: &Layered<'_>,
        schema: &Schema,
        viewer: &str,
        limits: Limits,
    ) -> Result<Plan, RuntimeError> {
        let mut plan = Plan {
            changes: Vec::new(),
            principals: BTreeMap::new(),
            total: self.total,
        };
        for key in layer.changed() {
            if let Some((owner, bytes)) = self.owners.get(&key) {
                plan.total.remove(*bytes);
                plan.principals
                    .entry(owner.clone())
                    .or_insert(self.principals[owner])
                    .remove(*bytes);
            }
            let owner = if layer.eph.get(&key.0, &key.1)?.is_some() {
                let size = declared_size(&schema.tables[&key.0]);
                plan.total.add(size);
                plan.principals
                    .entry(viewer.into())
                    .or_insert_with(|| self.principals.get(viewer).copied().unwrap_or_default())
                    .add(size);
                Some((viewer.into(), size))
            } else {
                None
            };
            plan.changes.push((key, owner));
        }
        if plan.total.rows > limits.rows
            || plan.total.bytes > limits.bytes
        Self {
            rows: MemoryRows::new(),
            identity,
            generation,
            tables: schema
                .tables
                .iter()
                .filter(|(_, t)| t.ephemeral.is_some())
                .map(|(n, t)| (n.clone(), t.clone()))
                .collect(),
            accounting: Accounting::default(),
            version: Version {
                epoch: 0,
                incarnation: incarnation(),
            },
            ring: VecDeque::new(),
            preserved: VecDeque::new(),
            publisher: None,
        }
    }
    pub fn version(&self) -> Version {
        self.version.clone()
    }
    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> Snapshot {
        Snapshot {
            identity: self.identity.clone(),
            generation: self.generation,
            version: self.version.clone(),
            ring: self.ring.clone(),
            preserved: self.preserved.clone(),
            tables: self.tables.keys().cloned().collect(),
        }
    }
    fn publish(&self) {
        if let Some(publish) = &self.publisher { publish(self.snapshot()); }
    }
    fn rebind(&mut self, identity: String, generation: u64, schema: &Schema) {
        let publisher = self.publisher.take();
        *self = Self::new(identity, generation, schema);
        self.publisher = publisher;
        self.publish();
    }
    // Snapshot identity has already been established by the deferred transaction.
    pub fn bind(
        &mut self,
        identity: &str,
        generation: u64,
        schema: &Schema,
    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if identity != self.identity || generation < self.generation {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation(
                "ephemeral hub serves a newer store or generation; retry with a fresh reader"
                    .into(),
            ));
        }
        if generation > self.generation {
            self.rebind(identity.into(), generation, schema);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
    fn sweep(&mut self, now: i64) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
        let mut removed = 0;
        for (name, table) in &self.tables {
            loop {
                let rows = self.rows.scan(
                    name,
                    "$expiry",
                    &[],
                    &Bounds {
                        lte: Some(Value::Int(now)),
                        ..Bounds::default()
                    },
                    Dir::Asc,
                    256,
                )?;
                if rows.is_empty() {
                    break;
                }
                for (row, _) in rows {
                    let id = row.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
                    self.rows.delete(name, table, &row)?;
                    self.accounting.remove(&(name.clone(), id));
                    removed += 1;
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(removed)
    }
}
impl Hub {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#137-wave-19-correction-admissible-starts-and-persistent-refutations — campaign copies preserve deadlines, quotas and epochs without retaining the live publisher.
    pub(super) fn fork_fixture(&self,identity:&str,generation:u64)->Result<Self,RuntimeError>{
        let state=self.lock()?;
        if state.identity!=identity||state.generation!=generation{return Err(RuntimeError::Schema("fixture fork needs a hub bound to the selected store generation".into()));}
        Ok(Self{limits:self.limits,state:Mutex::new(HubState{rows:state.rows.fork_fixture(),identity:state.identity.clone(),generation:state.generation,tables:state.tables.clone(),accounting:state.accounting.clone(),version:state.version.clone(),ring:state.ring.clone(),preserved:state.preserved.clone(),publisher:None})})
    }
    pub fn new(identity: String, generation: u64, schema: &Schema) -> Self {
        Self::with_limits(identity, generation, schema, Limits::default())
    }
    pub fn with_limits(identity: String, generation: u64, schema: &Schema, limits: Limits) -> Self {
        Self {
            state: Mutex::new(HubState::new(identity, generation, schema)),
            limits,
        }
    }
    pub fn lock(&self) -> Result<MutexGuard<'_, HubState>, RuntimeError> {
        self.state
            .lock()
            .map_err(|_| RuntimeError::Store("ephemeral hub lock poisoned".into()))
    }
    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> Result<Snapshot, RuntimeError> {
        Ok(self.lock()?.snapshot())
    }
    /// Install before serving. Called under the hub lock after each commit or
    /// rebind; the callback may take the wake mutex, never the hub or engine.
    // @ref LLP 3000.017#3.3 — publish before releasing the hub.
    pub fn set_publisher(&self, publisher: impl Fn(Snapshot) + Send + Sync + 'static) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let mut hub = self.lock()?;
        hub.publisher = Some(Arc::new(publisher));
        hub.publish();
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Write rows as the system principal: rules are bypassed, validation,
    /// constraints, aggregates and partition events are not. One commit.
    pub fn seed(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.seed_at(rows, crate::now_ms())
    }

    /// Development hosts supply their clock so seed commits share operation and stream time.
    pub fn seed_at(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>, now: i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        let now = self.advance_time_audience(now)?;
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let now = time_audience::pin_writer_time(&self.schema, &tx, now)?;
        let count = seed_rows(&self.schema, &tx, rows, now)?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(count)
    }

    /// Delete a row as the system principal: constraints, aggregates and
    /// partition events apply. Returns the number of rows removed (0 or 1).
    pub fn seed_delete(&mut self, table: &str, id: &str) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.seed_delete_at(table,id,crate::now_ms())
    }
    /// Trusted deletion with the fixture/host clock, including publication and expiry.
    pub fn seed_delete_at(&mut self, table: &str, id: &str, now:i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.schema.refuse_ephemeral_import(table)?;
        timeline::check_import(&self.schema, table)?;
        let now = self.advance_time_audience(now)?;
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let now = time_audience::pin_writer_time(&self.schema, &tx, now)?;
        let seq = tx.next_seq()?;
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(if crate::schema::live::has_live(&self.schema) {Ceilings::job()}else{Ceilings::unbounded()}, Cut::Abort), seq);
        if crate::schema::live::has_live(&self.schema) {runner.reserve_bulk_source(table)?;}
        let removed = match runner.delete(table, id) {
            Ok(()) => 1,
            Err(RuntimeError::Require { .. }) => 0,
            Err(error) => return Err(error),
        };
        let events = runner.events.take();
        let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
        drop(runner);
        if removed > 0 {
            tx.record_commit(seq, now, "seed", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "seed", &tables, removed)?;
            partition::append(&tx, seq, &events)?;
        }
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(removed)
    }

    /// Untrusted fixture import. All targets are installed before attribution
    /// is captured, so import order cannot invent dangling references.
    pub fn load(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.load_mode(rows, LoadMode::Import)
    }

    /// Restore trusted durable images, including timeline and attribution history.
    pub fn load_trusted(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.load_mode(rows, LoadMode::Restore)
    }

use super::*;
impl Store {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#122-reproducible-campaigns-and-reachability — fork committed state, never reinterpret stored rows as new seed input.
    pub fn fork_memory(&self) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let mut fork = Self::memory()?;
        {
            let backup = rusqlite::backup::Backup::new(&self.conn, &mut fork.conn)?;
            backup.run_to_completion(256, std::time::Duration::ZERO, None)?;
        }
        fork.catalog = self.catalog.clone();
        fork.identity = self.identity.clone();
        fork.server = self.server;
        if self.assets_dir.exists() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(&fork.assets_dir).map_err(io)?;
            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&self.assets_dir).map_err(io)? {
                let entry = entry.map_err(io)?;
                if !entry.file_type().map_err(io)?.is_file() {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Store("fixture asset ledger contains a non-file".into()));
                }
                std::fs::copy(entry.path(), fork.assets_dir.join(entry.file_name())).map_err(io)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(fork)
    }
}

fn io(e:std::io::Error)->RuntimeError {RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string())}

impl Store {
    /// Independent valid worlds share verified bytes, never source row state.
    pub fn empty_with_asset_ledger(&self)->Result<Self,RuntimeError>{
        let fresh=Self::memory()?;
        let ids=self.conn.prepare("SELECT id FROM _assets")?.query_map([],|r|r.get::<_,String>(0))?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>()?;
        for id in ids{
            let mut asset=self.asset(&id)?.ok_or_else(||RuntimeError::Store("fixture asset disappeared".into()))?;
            asset.home=None;asset.released_at=None;
            fresh.record_asset(&asset)?;
        }
        if self.assets_dir.exists(){
            std::fs::create_dir_all(&fresh.assets_dir).map_err(io)?;
            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&self.assets_dir).map_err(io)?{
                let entry=entry.map_err(io)?;
                if !entry.file_type().map_err(io)?.is_file(){return Err(RuntimeError::Store("fixture asset is not a regular file".into()));}
                std::fs::copy(entry.path(),fresh.assets_dir.join(entry.file_name())).map_err(io)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(fresh)
    }
}

#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize,serde::Deserialize,PartialEq,Eq)]
#[serde(rename_all="kebab-case")]
pub enum Reachability { AuthoredFixtureTrace, GeneratedFixtureTrace, TrustedSeedOnly, SyntheticOperation }
impl Reachability {
    pub fn is_reachable(&self)->bool {matches!(self,Self::AuthoredFixtureTrace|Self::GeneratedFixtureTrace)}
}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize,serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct Counterexample {pub reachability:Reachability,pub invariant_site:String,pub args:BTreeMap<String,Value>,pub viewer:String,pub operation:String,pub effect:String,pub row:Value,pub old:Option<Value>,pub supporting_rows:BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,pub rule_path:String,pub scope:String, pub observations:Option<paired::Observations>,pub history:Vec<histories::Step>}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Default,Serialize)]
pub struct Campaign {#[serde(skip)] pub history_limit:usize,#[serde(skip)] pub generated_fixture:bool,#[serde(skip)] pub work_remaining:Option<usize>,#[serde(skip)] pub deadline:Option<std::time::Instant>,pub paired_observer_calls:usize,pub history_observer_calls:usize,pub column_observer_calls:usize,pub operations:BTreeMap<String,usize>,pub sequences:usize,pub attempted:usize,pub admitted:usize,pub refused:usize,pub violations:Vec<Counterexample>,pub boundary_failures:Vec<String>,pub unsupported:BTreeSet<String>}
impl Campaign {
    pub fn bounded(milliseconds:u64)->Self {Self{work_remaining:Some(512),deadline:Some(std::time::Instant::now()+std::time::Duration::from_millis(milliseconds)),..Self::default()}}
    pub(super) fn reserve(&mut self,cost:usize)->bool {
        if self.exhausted(){return false;}
        if let Some(remaining)=&mut self.work_remaining {
            if *remaining<cost{*remaining=0;self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());return false;}
            *remaining-=cost;
        }true
    }
    pub(super) fn exhausted(&mut self)->bool {
        if self.work_remaining==Some(0){self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());return true;}
        if self.deadline.is_some_and(|deadline|std::time::Instant::now()>=deadline){self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration time budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());true}else{false}
    }
}
fn id(table:&str)->Value {Value::Id(format!("security-{table}"))}
fn sample(kind:&C,variant:usize)->Option<Value>{Some(match kind{
    C::Optional(inner)=>if variant&4!=0{sample(inner,variant).unwrap_or(Value::Null)}else{Value::Null},C::Id|C::Principal=>Value::Id(if variant&1==0{VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into()),
    C::Ref{table}=>id(table),C::Text{max}=>Value::String("x".repeat((*max).min(20) as usize)),
    C::Enum{variants}=>Value::String(variants.get(variant%variants.len())?.clone()),
    C::Int|C::Decimal{..}|C::Money{..}=>Value::Int(1),C::IntRange{min,max}=>Value::Int(1i64.clamp(*min,*max)),
    C::Bool=>Value::Bool((variant>>2)&1==0),C::Time=>Value::Int(NOW-1000),C::Json{..}=>Value::Null,C::Bytes{..}=>Value::Bytes(Vec::new()),
    C::List{..}=>Value::Array(Vec::new()),C::ListRange{of,min,..}=>Value::Array((0..*min).map(|_|sample(of,variant)).collect::<Option<_>>()?),
    C::Image{..}=>Value::String("$security:image".into()),C::File{..}=>Value::String("$security:file".into()),C::Video{..}=>return None,
})}
fn needed(schema:&Schema,name:&str,out:&mut BTreeSet<String>){
    if !out.insert(name.into()){return;}let Some(t)=schema.table(name)else{return};
    for m in schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m|m.target==name){needed(schema,&m.source,out);}
    for k in t.columns.values(){if let C::Ref{table}=k.required(){needed(schema,table,out);}}
    for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{for dep in kernel::dependencies(r){needed(schema,&dep,out);}}
}
fn snapshot(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    snapshot_mode(bound,inv,variant,false)
}
fn snapshot_mode(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize,timed:bool)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    let mut names=BTreeSet::new();needed(&bound.schema,&inv.table,&mut names);
    if let Some(policy)=&inv.policy{for dep in kernel::dependencies(policy){needed(&bound.schema,&dep,&mut names);}}
    if let Some(mut syntax)=inv.syntax.clone(){super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut syntax,&mut |e| {
        if let ast::Expr::Read(r)|ast::Expr::Exists{read:r,..}=e {needed(&bound.schema,&r.table,&mut names);}
    });}
    if let Some(op)=&inv.operation && let Some(p)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==op){for kind in p.program.args.values(){if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind{needed(&bound.schema,table,&mut names);}}}
    let mut out=BTreeMap::new();
    let mut enum_case=variant;
    for name in names {
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&name];
        if bound.schema.maintain_for(&name).is_some(){continue;}
        let mut fields=BTreeMap::new();fields.insert("id".into(),id(&name));
        let mut principal_index=0usize;
        for (n,k) in &t.columns{
            if n=="id"||t.engine_field(n)||t.maintained.contains_key(n){continue;}
            let sample_variant=if let C::Enum{variants}=k.required(){let n=enum_case % variants.len();enum_case/=variants.len();n}else if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal){let n=((variant>>((if name==inv.table{0}else{3})+principal_index.min(5)))&1)|(variant&4);principal_index+=1;n}else{variant};
            let value=sample(k,sample_variant)
                .ok_or_else(||format!("{name}.{n}: asset ledger/required sample is not available"))?;
            fields.insert(n.clone(),value);
        }
        if timed || t.read_lifetime_sensitive() {for (n,k) in &t.columns {if matches!(k.required(),C::Time)&&(!k.is_optional()||variant&4!=0) {
            fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Int(NOW+if t.expire.as_ref()==Some(n){2000}else if timed{1000}else{-1000}));
        }}}
        for constraint in &t.constraints{if let Constraint::Text{field,min,..}=constraint
            && matches!(fields.get(field),Some(Value::String(_))) {fields.insert(field.clone(),Value::String("x".repeat((*min).max(1) as usize)));}}
        // Named primary keys used by role probes must select the challenged
        // viewer; changing the enum represents a current-role change while
        // derived assignment/membership rows remain present.
        if name!=inv.table {for (i,(n,k)) in t.columns.iter().filter(|(_,k)|matches!(k,C::Principal)).enumerate(){fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Id(if variant & (1 << (i+3)) == 0 {VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into())); let _=k;}}
        out.insert(name,vec![Value::Row(fields.into())]);
    }
    Ok(out)
}
struct Snapshot<'a>{schema:&'a Schema,rows:&'a BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>}
impl Probe for Snapshot<'_>{
    fn exists(&mut self,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[Value],equalities:&[(String,Value)])->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{
        let t=&self.schema.tables[table];let Some(idx)=t.index(index)else{return Ok(false)};
        if idx.components.len()!=key.len(){return Ok(false);}
        if idx.unique&&idx.components.iter().any(|c|c.column().is_some_and(|n|t.columns.get(n).is_some_and(C::is_optional)))&&key.contains(&Value::Null){return Ok(false);}
        Ok(self.rows.get(table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|r|live(t,r)).any(|r|idx.components.iter().zip(key).all(|(c,v)|c.column().is_some_and(|n|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v)))&&equalities.iter().all(|(n,v)|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v))))
    }
    fn created(&mut self,_:&str,_:&str,_:&[Value])->bool{false}

}
fn live(t:&snapback4_core::schema::Table,row:&Value)->bool{
    !t.expired(row,NOW)&&t.not_before.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|match row.field(c){Ok(Value::Int(n))=>*n<=NOW,Ok(Value::Null)=>true,_=>false})
}
fn policy(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value)->Result<bool,String>{

    let Some(p)=inv.policy.as_ref() else{return inv.syntax.as_ref().and_then(|e|super::concrete::evaluate(e,row,rows,&bound.schema,viewer)).ok_or_else(||"concrete policy expression is unknown/unsupported (including clock and typed literal coercions)".into());};

    let mut image=row.clone();if super::temporal::has_clock(p)&&let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image{fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(NOW));}
    if p.has_reference(){return Err("reference intent requires authorized engine policy replay".into());}
    rules::evaluate(p,viewer,old,&image,false,&mut Snapshot{schema:&bound.schema,rows}).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
}
fn policy_in(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,engine:&mut Engine)->Result<bool,String>{
    let Some(p)=&inv.policy else{return policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row);};
    if !p.has_reference(){return policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row);}
    let mut image=row.clone();if super::temporal::has_clock(p)&&let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image{fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(NOW));}
    match engine.fixture_policy(p,viewer,old,&image,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?{
        rules::Verdict::Readable=>Ok(true),rules::Verdict::Denied=>Ok(false),rules::Verdict::Unknown=>Err("policy reference/coverage judgment is unknown; never a refutation".into()),

    fn write_row_with_id(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, fields: &[ObjectField], id: Expr, site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let id = self.snapshot(id, out);
        let mut exprs = vec![("id".to_owned(), id)];
        let mut given = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        for field in fields {
            let Some(scalar) = info.columns.get(&field.name) else {
                return Err(unknown(&field.site, format!("{} has no column {}", info.decl.name, field.name), name_rewrite(&field.name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str), None)));
            };
            let expected = Ty::from_scalar(scalar);
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let (e, t) = self.field_value(field, &expected, &mut setup)?;
            literal_in_range(info, &field.name, scalar, &e, &field.site)?;
            self.sequence_setup(exprs.iter_mut().map(|(_, e)| e), setup, out);
            if !t.writable_to(&expected) {
                return Err(diagnostics::write_field_type(info, field, &expected, &t));
            }
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            if !given.insert(field.name.clone()) {
                return Err(unknown(&field.site, format!("field {} is written twice", field.name), "keep one"));
            }
            exprs.push((field.name.clone(), e));
        }
        // Materialize the same defaults in the insert expression so its returned row is the written image.
        for column in &info.decl.columns {
            if !given.contains(&column.name) && let Some(default) = &column.default {
                if column.written_by.is_some() || self.schema.receipt_assigned(&info.decl.name, &column.name) {
                    // Preserve omission until the engine checks supplied-field authority.
                    given.insert(column.name.clone());
                    continue;
                }
                let default = super::defaults::column(column, default)?;
                let value = match default {
                    snapback4_core::schema::ColumnDefault::Literal(value) => Expr::Literal(value),
                    snapback4_core::schema::ColumnDefault::Now => Expr::Input(Input::Now),
                    snapback4_core::schema::ColumnDefault::Viewer => Expr::Input(Input::Viewer),
                };
                exprs.push((column.name.clone(), value));
                given.insert(column.name.clone());
            }
        }
        let missing: Vec<&str> = info.columns.iter().filter(|(name, scalar)| !scalar.optional && !self.schema.receipt_assigned(&info.decl.name, name) && !info.decl.columns.iter().any(|c| c.name == **name && c.sequence.is_some()) && !super::columns::fields(info).any(|f|f == *name) && !given.contains(*name)).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect();
        if !missing.is_empty() {
            return Err(type_error(site, format!("insert into {} omits required column{} {}", info.decl.name, if missing.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, missing.join(", ")), format!("insert {} {{ {} }}", info.decl.name, given.iter().cloned().chain(missing.iter().map(|m| format!("{m}: <{}>", info.columns.get(*m).map(print::scalar_type).unwrap_or_default()))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))));
        }
        Ok((Expr::Object(exprs.into_iter().collect()), self.write_result_type(info)))
    }

    fn project_insert(&self, info: &TableInfo, row: Expr) -> Expr {
        if info.decl.columns.iter().any(|column| column.written_by.is_some()) {
            Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::ProjectFields { table: info.decl.name.clone() }, args: vec![row] }
        } else { row }
    }

    fn write_result_type(&self, info: &TableInfo) -> Ty {
        if super::columns::fields(info).next().is_some() { Ty::WriteRow(info.decl.name.clone()) } else { Ty::Row(info.decl.name.clone()) }
    }
